. We include the details for completion.

Proof.

Items
1-5
come from the original statement, which assumes unit variance. Here our variance parameter
\rho
simply induces a multiplicative change. We now focus on item
6
.

Let
\zeta=\xi/\eta=1/\eta+{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}
. With
{\bm{A}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}
(SVD),
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times n}
having i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\rho^{2}/d)
entries, and
{\bm{u}},{\bm{v}}
fixed unit vectors, we have
\zeta=\frac{1}{\eta}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}}b_{i}a_{i}
, where
r=\min(d,n)
,
{\bm{a}}={\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}
,
{\bm{b}}={\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}
are uniformly random on
S^{n-1}
and
S^{d-1}
respectively since
{\bm{U}}
,
{\bm{V}}
are random rotations.

Since
{\bm{A}}
has zero-mean entries, only the non-cross terms remain in the expectation, and the fourth moment is

\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{4}]=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+\frac{6}{\eta^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}\right]\mathbb{E}[b_{i}b_{j}]\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}]+\sum_{i,j,k,l}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{l}}\right]\mathbb{E}[b_{i}b_{j}b_{k}b_{l}]\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}a_{k}a_{l}].

Furthermore, non-zero expectation terms require paired indices (since odd moments of the uniformly random vector on the sphere equals
0
). In particular, using exact spherical moments, we have
\mathbb{E}[a_{i}^{4}]=\frac{3}{n(n+2)}
,
\mathbb{E}[a_{i}^{2}]=\frac{1}{n}
,
\mathbb{E}[a_{i}^{2}a_{j}^{2}]=\frac{1}{n(n+2)}
(
i\neq j
),
\mathbb{E}[b_{i}^{4}]=\frac{3}{d(d+2)}
,
\mathbb{E}[b_{i}^{2}]=\frac{1}{d}
,
\mathbb{E}[b_{i}^{2}b_{j}^{2}]=\frac{1}{d(d+2)}
(
i\neq j
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{4}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+\frac{6}{\eta^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\right]\frac{1}{dn}+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}}\right]\frac{9}{d(d+2)n(n+2)}+3\sum_{i\neq k}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}\sigma_{k}^{2}}\right]\frac{1}{d(d+2)n(n+2)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+\underbrace{\frac{9\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{4}]}{d(d+2)n(n+2)}}_{\text{$I_{1}$}}+\underbrace{\frac{3\sum_{i\neq k}\mathbb{E}[1/(\sigma_{i}^{2}\sigma_{k}^{2})]}{d(d+2)n(n+2)}}_{\text{$I_{2}$}}+\underbrace{\frac{6}{\eta^{2}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{2}]}{dn}}_{\text{$I_{3}$}}.

Leading Order Scaling and Mean.

Let
N=\max(d,n)
, assume
n,d\to\infty
with
d/n\to c\neq 1
. Lemma 5 from
[
32
]
implies that if
{\bm{A}}
has unit variance entries, the moments of its inverse eigenvalue are expressions of
c
and are hence
O(1)
. In our case, it will just scale with
\rho
instead:

\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{4}]=O(1/\rho^{4}),\quad\mathbb{E}[1/(\sigma_{i}^{2}\sigma_{k}^{2})]=O(1/\rho^{4}),\quad\text{and }\ \ \ \mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{8}]=O(1/\rho^{8})\ \ \text{ etc}.

In particular, we also need the following exact expectation from the same lemma:

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\right]=\frac{c}{\rho^{2}|1-c|}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

(8)

Since the above
I_{1}
,
I_{3}
have
r=\min(d,n)
summands, this implies

I_{1}=O\left(\frac{r}{N^{4}\rho^{4}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{3}\rho^{4}}\right),\quad I_{3}=O\left(\frac{r}{\eta^{2}N^{2}\rho^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{N\rho^{4}}\right).

Similarly,
I_{2}
has
r(r-1)\approx r^{2}
summands, and

I_{2}=O\left(\frac{r^{2}}{N^{4}\rho^{4}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}\rho^{4}}\right)

\implies\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{4}]=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\max(d,n)\rho^{4}}\right)\quad\text{since $I_{3}$ dominates}.

(9)

With a similar expansion for the second moment and taking spherical moments, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{2}]=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}\right]\mathbb{E}[b_{i}b_{j}]\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{2}]}{dn}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+\frac{\min(d,n)}{dn}\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}|1-c|}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)\text{ by Equation \ref{eq:one_over_lambda}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+\frac{1}{\max(d,n)}\frac{c}{\rho^{2}|1-c|}+o\left(\frac{1}{\max(d,n)\rho^{2}}\right).

This gives us the mean. Furthermore,

(\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{2}])^{2}=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+\frac{2}{\eta^{2}}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{2}]}{dn}+\frac{(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}[1/\sigma_{i}^{2}])^{2}}{d^{2}n^{2}}=\frac{1}{\eta^{4}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\max(d,n)\rho^{4}}\right).

(10)

Variance.

\mathrm{Var}(\zeta^{2})=\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{4}]-(\mathbb{E}[\zeta^{2}])^{2}
. From Equations
9
,
10
, the overall scaling is determined by the dominant term:

\mathrm{Var}\left(\left(\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right)^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\max(d,n)\rho^{4}}\right).

∎

Lemma 10

(General Terms)
.

In the setting of
Section

2
we have the following expectations:

1.

For
c<1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\frac{c}{\rho^{2}(1-c)}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)
and the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{4}d))
.

2.

For
c<1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}}]=\frac{c^{2}}{\rho^{4}(1-c)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)
and the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{8}d))
.

3.

For
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\frac{c-1}{c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}
and the variance is
O(1/d)
.

4.

For
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\frac{c-1}{c^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)
and the variance is
O(1/d)
.

5.

For
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\frac{c}{\rho^{2}(c-1)}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}d}\right)
and the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{4}d^{2}))
.

6.

For
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}]=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)
and the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{8}d))
.

7.

For
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}]=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)
and the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{4}n))

Proof.

For all these terms, we evaluate the expectation using the SVD
{\bm{A}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}
, with
{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}={\bm{V}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{U}}^{\top}
, and important expectations from Lemma 5 of
[
32
]
regarding the spectrum of
{\bm{A}}
: suppose
\tilde{{\bm{A}}}
has unit variance (general
\rho^{2}
is a multiplicative change), and let
\sigma_{i}(\tilde{{\bm{A}}})
denote the
i
-th singular value. We have

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}(\tilde{{\bm{A}}})}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{c}{1-c}+o(1)&c<1\\
\frac{c}{c-1}+o(1)&c>1\end{cases},\quad\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}(\tilde{{\bm{A}}})}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{c^{2}}{(1-c)^{3}}+o(1)&c<1\\
\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o(1)&c>1\end{cases}.

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}({{\bm{A}}})}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)&c<1\\
\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)&c>1\end{cases},\quad\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}({{\bm{A}}})}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{2}}{(1-c)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)&c<1\\
\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)&c>1\end{cases}.

(11)

For the first term
, we note that

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}A^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}){\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}){\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\sum_{i=1}^{d}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})_{i}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}({\bm{A}})}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\sum_{i=1}^{d}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}({\bm{A}})},

where
{\bm{u}}_{i}
denotes the
i
-th column of
{\bm{U}}
. We further note that
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
. Since permuting columns of an orthogonal matrix does not break orthogonality and
{\bm{U}}
is uniformly random, we have that the marginals
{\bm{u}}_{i}
are identical. Thus, we have that

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{1}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{1}]=\ldots=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{d}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{d}]=\frac{1}{d}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})\quad\text{since $\mathbb{E}[{\bm{u}}_{i}{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\top}]=\frac{1}{d}{\bm{I}}$}.

It follows from here that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}({\bm{A}})}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{d}\left(\frac{c}{1-c}+o(1)\right)\quad\text{by Equation \ref{eq:inverse_eig}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

Since
{\bm{A}}
is isotropic Gaussian, we have that
{\bm{U}},{\bm{V}}
are uniformly random orthogonal matrices. Thus,
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}
and
{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
are uniformly random vectors on the spheres of radius
\|{\bm{u}}\|
and
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|
respectively.

Hence, when we consider the squared terms to compute the variance, the term from the two uniform vectors will contribute
O(1/d^{2})
. Together with the singular value term (now squared to have
O(1/\rho^{4})
) and the summation, the variance is of order
O(1/(\rho^{4}d))
.

For the second term
, we have that by Equation
11
,

\displaystyle{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\top}(({\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\top})^{\dagger})^{2}{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}(({\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top})^{\dagger})^{2}{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}({\bm{A}})},

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}}]=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}[({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}(A)}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{1}{d}\left(\frac{c^{2}}{(1-c)^{3}}+o(1)\right)=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{2}}{(1-c)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right),

where we again use
\mathbb{E}[({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})^{2}]=1/d
since it is the entry of a uniformly random vector of length
\|{\bm{u}}\|=1
.

Similarly, the variance is
O(1/(\rho^{8}d))
from the summation of
d
independent variances each of
O(1/(\rho^{8}d^{2}))
.

For the third term
, we have that

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}-({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i}).

Similarly, we take the expectation (in particular,
\mathbb{E}[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})]=1/d({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})
) and have

({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[1-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{d}\right]=\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}.

The variance for this term is
O(1/d)
from summation of
n=d/c
terms of
O(1/d^{2})
.

For the fourth term
, we plug in
{\bm{s}}=({\bm{I}}-{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}
and have

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}){\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}-({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}})^{2}.

From previous calculations, we have that

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i})\right]=\frac{1}{c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}).

Using
2
and this result, we can then show

\mathbb{E}[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}})^{2}]=\frac{1}{c^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1).

It follows that

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\frac{c-1}{c^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1).

The variance for this term is
O(1/d)
, where the dominant term is a summation of
n=d/c
terms of
O(1/d^{2})
.

For the fifth term
, we have

{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{v}})^{2}=\sum_{i,j}^{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})\sigma_{j}({\bm{A}})}({\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{i}({\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{j}.

Since
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}}
(and
{\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}
) are uniformly random and independent of everything else, we only have the diagonal terms when we take the expectation. By Equation
11
,

\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\frac{1}{n}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\left(\frac{c}{c-1}+o(1)\right)=\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}d}\right)

The variance for this term is
O(1/(\rho^{4}d^{2}))
from
O(d^{2})
terms of individual variances of
O(1/(\rho^{4}d^{4}))
.

For the sixth term
, by expansion and Equation
11
, similar to above,

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{i}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}({\bm{A}})}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{4}({\bm{A}})}\right]=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right).

The variance is
O\left(1/(\rho^{8}d)\right)
.

For the final term
, by expansion and Equation
11
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}^{2}({\bm{A}})}\right]=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{n}{d}\frac{c}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)

The variance is
O\left(1/(\rho^{4}n)\right)
.
∎

Lemma 11

(Zero Expectation)
.

In the setting of
Section

2
, we have the following expectations for

1.

\forall c
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})=O(1/(\rho^{2}d))

2.

If
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})=O(1/(\rho^{2}d^{2}))

3.

If
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})=O(1/(\rho^{2}d))

4.

\forall c
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}({\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top})=O(1/(\rho^{6}d))

5.

If
c>1
,
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}]=0
and
\mathrm{Var}({\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})=O(1/(\rho^{2}d))

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
10
, for all these terms, we evaluate the expectation using the SVD
{\bm{A}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}
, with
{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}={\bm{V}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{U}}^{\top}
.

For the first term
, we note that

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}){\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}){\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{V}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n,d)}({\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{V}})_{i}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})_{i}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})}.

Since
{\bm{A}}
is isotropic Gaussian, again we have that
{\bm{U}},{\bm{V}}
are uniformly random orthogonal matrices. Thus,
{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{V}}
and
{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
are uniformly random vectors on a spheres of radius
\|{\bm{v}}\|
and
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|
respectively. In particular, they are independent and have mean zero, which implies

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\right]=0.

The variance will be
O(1/(\rho^{2}d))
as a summation of
O(d)
terms of
O(1/(\rho^{2}d^{2}))
.

For the second term
, we note that

{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n,d)}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{u}})_{i}\quad\text{ and }\quad{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n,d)}({\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{V}})_{i}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})_{i}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})}

Multiplying the two together yields

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=\sum_{i,j}^{\min(n,d)}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{u}})_{i}({\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{V}})_{j}({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})_{j}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})}.

We note that
v^{\top}V
is a uniformly random mean zero vector independent of everything else in the summation. Hence, the expectation is equal to zero, and similar to Lemma
, the variance of this term is
O(1/(\rho^{2}d^{2}))
(a summation of
O(d^{2})
terms of
O(1/(\rho^{2}d^{4}))
).

For the third term
, we have that

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{I}}-{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}.

Then using the previous two parts, we get that each term has mean zero. Thus, we get the needed result. Using
Lemma

34
and the first two terms, the variance of this term is
O(1/(\rho^{2}d))
.

For the fourth term
, we have that:

{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}={\bm{u}}{\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\min(n,d)}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}\,({\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{i}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})^{3}}.

Similarly, using the independence of
{\bm{U}},{\bm{\Sigma}},{\bm{V}}
and uniformly random entries, we get mean zero and variance
O(1/(\rho^{6}d))
.

For the last term
, we have that:

{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=\sum_{i=\min(n,d)}^{r}({\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}})_{i}\,({\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*})_{i}\frac{1}{\sigma_{i}({\bm{A}})}.

Using the independence of
{\bm{U}},{\bm{\Sigma}},{\bm{V}}
and uniformly random entries, we get mean zero and variance
O(1/(\rho^{2}d))
.
∎

D.4.2
Step 3(b): Bounding the Higher Moments

To bound the higher moments, we will the following Gaussian hypercontractivity lemma.

Lemma 12

(Gaussian Hypercontractivity Inequality)
.

Let
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
be a standard Gaussian random variable. Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a degree
k
polynomial.
Then, for any
q\geq 2
, the
L_{q}
norm of
f(G)
is bounded by its
L_{2}
norm as follows:

\|f(G)\|_{L_{q}}\leq(q-1)^{k/2}\|f(G)\|_{L_{2}},

where the
L_{p}
norm of a random variable
X
is defined as
\|X\|_{L_{p}}=(\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}])^{1/p}
.

Proof.

Follows directly from
[
24
, Lemma 20]
.
∎

Lemma 13

(Multivariate Gaussian Hypercontractivity)
.

Let
G=(G_{1},\ldots,G_{M})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{M})
and let
P:\mathbb{R}^{M}\to\mathbb{R}
be a polynomial of total degree
r
. Consider the Hermite expansion of
P

P(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{m},|\alpha|\leq r}c_{\alpha}{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x).

with coefficient random and independent of
G
. Then there exists a constant
C
that is only dependent on
M,r
such that for any
q\geq 2
,

\|P(G)\|_{L_{q}}\leq C(q-1)^{r/2}\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}^{2}\alpha!\right)^{1/2}

Further, if for all
|\alpha|\leq r
, we have that
\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}^{2}\leq C_{q}^{2}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}
, then

\|P(G)\|_{L_{q}}\leq C(q-1)^{r/2}\|P(G)\|_{L_{2}}

Where the
L_{p}
norm is over all of the randomness. Furthermore,

Proof.

Let
H_{k}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be the probabilist Hermite polynomial. Given
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{M}
, define

{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x):=\prod_{j=1}^{M}H_{\alpha_{j}}(x_{j})

Then since
P
is degree
r
, then we can decompose

P(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{m},|\alpha|\leq r}c_{\alpha}{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x).

Here
|\alpha|=\sum_{j}\alpha_{j}
. Since the Hermite polynomials are orthogonal, we can see that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x){\bm{H}}_{\tilde{\alpha}}(x)\gamma_{M}(x)=\delta_{\alpha\tilde{\alpha}}\prod_{j=1}^{M}\alpha_{j}!,

where
\gamma_{M}
is the density for an
M
-dimensional standard normal distribution.

\displaystyle\|P(x)\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{M}}|P(x)|^{2}\gamma_{M}(x)dx\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\sum_{|\tilde{\alpha}|\leq r}\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\Sigma}}}\left[c_{\alpha}c_{\tilde{\alpha}}\right]\int{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x){\bm{H}}_{\tilde{\alpha}}(x)\gamma_{M}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}\alpha!

where
\displaystyle\alpha!:=\prod_{j=1}^{M}\alpha_{j}!
.

Then using the 1D Gaussian Hypercontractivity (Lemma
12
, we see that

\displaystyle\|{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)\|_{L_{q}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{M}\|H_{\alpha_{j}}(x_{j})\|_{L_{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{M}(q-1)^{\alpha_{j}/2}\|H_{\alpha_{j}}(x_{j})\|_{L_{2}}

\displaystyle=(q-1)^{|\alpha|/2}\prod_{j=1}^{M}\sqrt{\alpha_{j}!}

\displaystyle=(q-1)^{|\alpha|/2}\sqrt{\alpha!}

Thus, using the triangle inequality we get that

\|P(x)\|_{L_{q}}\leq\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)\|_{L_{q}}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}\|{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)\|_{L_{q}}

Thus

\|P(x)\|_{L_{q}}\leq\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)\|_{L_{q}}\leq\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}(q-1)^{|\alpha|/2}\sqrt{\alpha!}\leq(q-1)^{r/2}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}\sqrt{\alpha!}

Then using Cauchy-Schwartz, we get that

\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}\sqrt{\alpha!}\leq\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}^{2}\alpha!\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}1\right)^{1/2}.

Finally, we note that

C_{M,r}:=\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}1\right)^{1/2}

is some universal constant that only depends on
M,r
. Thus, we get that

\|P(x)\|_{L_{q}}\leq C_{M,r}\,(q-1)^{r/2}\,\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}^{2}\alpha!\right)^{1/2}

Using the assumption

\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}^{2}\leq C_{q}^{2}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{2}}^{2}

Then we get

\|P(x)\|_{L_{q}}\leq C_{M,r}C_{q}\,(q-1)^{r/2}\,\|P(x)\|_{L_{2}}

∎

Lemma 14

(Product Spherical Hypercontractivity)
.

Let
l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}\geq 0
, let
\Theta_{1}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(S^{l_{1}})
,
\Theta_{2}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(S^{l_{2}})
,
\Theta_{3}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(S^{l_{3}})
be independent, and let
H:\mathbb{R}^{l_{1}+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{l_{2}+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{l_{3}+1}\to\mathbb{R}
be a multi-homogeneous polynomial of total degree
r
. Then for every
q\geq 2
,

\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{q}}\ \leq\ C_{r,q}(q-1)^{r/2}\,\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{2}},

where the norms are with respect to the product measure. For homogeneous polynomials, the constant is independent of the dimension.

Proof.

H
is multi-homogeneous of degrees
r_{1},r_{2},r_{3}
with
r_{1}+r_{2}+r_{3}=r
. Let
G_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{l_{1}+1})
,
G_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{l_{2}+1})
,
G_{3}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{l_{3}+1})
be independent with polar decompositions
G_{i}=R_{i}\Theta_{i}
, where the
R_{i}
’s are independent of each other and of the
\Theta_{i}
’s. Then

H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})=R_{1}^{r_{1}}R_{2}^{r_{2}}R_{3}^{r_{3}}H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3}),

so for any
p>0
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})\right|^{p}\right]=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{pr_{i}}\right]\right)\,\mathbb{E}\left[\left|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\right|^{p}\right]

Then we have that

\left\|H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})\right\|_{L_{p}}=\left(\prod_{i}(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{pr_{i}}\right])^{1/p}\right)\,\left\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\right\|_{L_{p}}.

(12)

Apply Gaussian hypercontractivity (Lemma
12
) to
H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})
(total degree
r
):

\|H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})\|_{L_{q}}\ \leq\ C(q-1)^{r/2}\,\|H(G_{1},G_{2},G_{3})\|_{L_{2}},\qquad q\geq 2.

Using Equation
12
with
p=q
and
p=2
yields

\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{q}}\ \leq\ C(q-1)^{r/2}\,\left(\prod_{i}\frac{(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{2r_{i}}\right])^{1/2}}{(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{qr_{i}}\right])^{1/q}}\right)\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{2}}.

For each
i
, since
q\geq 2
and
R_{i}\geq 0
, monotonicity of
L_{p}
norms implies

(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{qr_{i}}\right])^{1/(qr_{i})}\geq(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{2r_{i}}\right])^{1/(2r_{i})}
, hence

\frac{(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{2r_{i}}\right])^{1/2}}{(\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i}^{qr_{i}}\right])^{1/q}}\ \leq\ 1.

Thus the product is less than 1, so

\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{q}}\ \leq\ C(q-1)^{r/2}\,\|H(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})\|_{L_{2}}.

∎

Lemma 15

(Product spherical hypercontractivity with random coefficients)
.

Let
l_{1},l_{2},l_{3}\geq 0
and let
\Theta_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(S^{l_{i}})
be independent. Let
r\in\mathbb{N}
and let
H:\mathbb{R}^{l_{1}+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{l_{2}+1}\times\mathbb{R}^{l_{3}+1}\to\mathbb{R}
be a multi-homogeneous polynomial of total degree at most
r
. Suppose the coefficients of
P
are random on an auxiliary probability space and are independent of
(\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\Theta_{3})
. If the random coefficients satisfy
\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{q}}\leq K_{q}\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{2}}
in the Hermite basis expansion, then for all
q\geq 2
:

\|H\|_{L_{q}}\leq C_{r,q}\,(q-1)^{r/2}\,\|H\|_{L_{2}}.

Proof.

The proof is identical to that of Lemma
14
, except we begin with the version of Gaussian hypercontractivity that handles random coefficients satisfying the stated assumption.
∎

Recall

{\bm{a}}:={\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,\quad{\bm{b}}:={\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\text{ and }\quad{\bm{u}}_{\beta}={\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

Then, since
{\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}
are fixed, and
{\bm{U}},{\bm{V}}
are independent Haar orthogonal matrices, we have that
{\bm{a}},{\bm{b}}
are all uniformly random vectors on their respective spheres. Additionally, using the assumption that
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
is uniformly random such that
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}
is constant.
{\bm{u}}_{\beta}
is uniformly random on a sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-2}
.

Consider the following centered versions and polynomial representations.

1.

\displaystyle Y_{h}:=\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}\right]={\bm{a}}^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}-\mu_{h}\right){\bm{a}}

2.

\displaystyle Y_{k}:=\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right]={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}-\mu_{k}\right){\bm{b}}

3.

\displaystyle Y_{t}:=\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\right]={\bm{a}}^{\top}\left((I-{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\Sigma}})-\mu_{t}\right)

4.

\displaystyle Y_{s}:=\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left((I-{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger})-\mu_{t}\right){\bm{b}}

5.

\displaystyle Y_{\xi}:=\frac{\xi}{\eta}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right]={\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{b}}={\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{b}}

6.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{1}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\,{\bm{b}}^{\top}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}_{\beta}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{1}}({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{b}})

7.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{2}:={\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}}\right]={\bm{b}}^{\top}\left(\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}\right)^{2}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{2}}\right){\bm{b}}

8.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{3}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\,{\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}(I-{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}){\bm{b}}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{3}}({\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{\beta})

9.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{4}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{s}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]={\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{b}}{\bm{b}}^{\top}(I-{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}_{\beta}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{4}}({\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{b}})({\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{\beta})

10.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{5}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=\left({\bm{u}}_{\beta}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{a}}\right)^{2}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{5}}({\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{a}})({\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{\beta})

11.

\displaystyle\tilde{T}_{6}:={\bm{h}}({\bm{A}}^{\dagger})^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{h}}({\bm{A}}^{\dagger})^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]={\bm{a}}^{\top}\left(\left({\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}\right)^{2}-\mu_{\tilde{T}_{6}}\right){\bm{a}}

12.

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{1}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})\,{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}_{\beta}

13.

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{2}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]={\bm{u}}_{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{b}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}_{\beta}

14.

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{3}:={\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{s}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]={\bm{u}}_{\beta}(I-{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}){\bm{b}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}_{\beta}

15.

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{4}:={\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}-\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]={\bm{b}}^{\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{a}}

Hence we see that these are all homogeneous polynomials in uniformly random spherical variables. Thus, we can use Lemma
14
, we get bounds on the higher moments. In particular, since the coefficients are only dependent on constants and
{\bm{\Sigma}}
, we see that the coefficients are independent of
{\bm{a}},{\bm{b}},{\bm{u}}_{\beta}
. Then using a change of basis we see that that coefficients of the decomposition are also random and independent of the input variables. Finally, since the spectrum converges to the Marchenko-Pastur, we have that the coefficients have bounded moments. Hence the second assumption is satisfied.

D.4.3
Step 3(c): Bounding
\gamma_{i}
moments.

Lemma 16

(Moments of
\gamma_{i}/\eta^{2}
)
.

We have:

(i)

For
\gamma_{1}/\eta^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right]=\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right).

(ii)

For
\gamma_{2}/\eta^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right).

Proof.

We decompose

\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\eta^{2}}=\zeta_{i}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}},\quad i=1,2,\quad\text{where }\zeta_{1}=\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\,\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2},\quad\zeta_{2}=\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\,\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}.

Expectation Estimates:
We begin by noting that
\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}
depends only on
{\bm{V}}
and is independent of
{\bm{U}},{\bm{\Sigma}}
.
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
depends only on
{\bm{U}}
and is independent of
{\bm{V}},{\bm{\Sigma}}
. Additionally,
\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}
depends on
{\bm{U}}
and
{\bm{\Sigma}}
, hence is independent of
{\bm{V}}
. Also
\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}
depends on
{\bm{V}}
and
{\bm{\Sigma}}
and is independent of
{\bm{U}}
, hence is independent of
{\bm{U}}
.

Thus, we have have that
\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}
and
\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}
are independent and
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
and
\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}
are independent. Thus, we see that

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{1}]=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\,\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}]\;\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}].

Using
Lemma

9
again,

\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}]=1-c,\quad\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}]=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

We plug them into the expectation and get:

\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{1}]=\left(1-c\right)\left[\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}\right)+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right]=\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

Finally, we also have that from Lemma
9
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right),

Hence,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\zeta_{1}]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]=\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

A similar argument applies for
\gamma_{2}/\eta^{2}
, using the corresponding results for
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
,
\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}
.

Variance Estimates:

Again using independence, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2})\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2})+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}]^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2})+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}]^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\,O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)+(1-c)^{2}\,O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)+\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{2}}{(1-c)^{2}}\,O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right).

We then use Lemma
34
to compute the variance of the sum:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\zeta_{1}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(\zeta_{1})}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)}+\sqrt{O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right).

This proof is similar to the other case.
∎

Lemma 17

(Moments of
(\gamma_{i}/\eta^{2})^{2}
)
.

We have, as
n,d\to\infty
with
d/n\to c\neq 1
,

(i)

For
\gamma_{1}/\eta^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\right]=\left(\frac{c}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\;+\;O\!\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right),\qquad\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}\,n}\right).

(ii)

For
\gamma_{2}/\eta^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\right]=\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\;+\;O\!\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right),\qquad\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\left(\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)^{\!2}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}\,n}\right).

Proof.

Write, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\eta^{2}}=\zeta_{i}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}},\qquad\zeta_{1}:=\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\,\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2},\ \ \zeta_{2}:=\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\,\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}.

Means.
Using Lemma
16
and the fact that for any random variable

\mathbb{E}\left[Y^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}[Y]^{2}+\mathrm{Var}(Y)

we get the means.

Variances.
Using

Y^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right]^{2}+2(\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right])\left(Y-\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right]\right)+\left(Y-\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right]\right)^{2},

Thus, using Lemma
34
we have that

\mathrm{Var}(Y^{2})\leq\left(\sqrt{4\left(\mathbb{E}\left[X\right]\right)^{2}\,\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})}\ +\ \sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\left(Y-\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right]\right)^{2}\right)}\right)^{2}.

By spherical hypercontractivity for degree-
4
polynomials,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right]\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right)^{2},

hence

\mathrm{Var}\left(\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right]\right)^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right]\right)^{4}\right]\lesssim\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right)^{2}.

Using
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\eta^{2}}\right]^{2}=O(1)
and
\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}}{\eta^{2}}\right)=O(\rho^{-4}n^{-1})
gives

\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{i}^{2}}{\eta^{4}}\right)=O\!\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right),

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 18

(Finite Negative Moments of
\gamma_{i}
)
.

Fix
p>0
. There exists an
N(p)
such that for all
n,d\geq N(p)
, we have that for
c<1

\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma_{1}^{-p}\right]\leq\eta^{-2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{1}^{2p}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T^{-p}\right]\leq\frac{\rho^{2p}}{\eta^{2p}}\,M^{p}

and for
c>1
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma_{2}^{-p}\right]\leq\eta^{-2p}\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{1}^{2p}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[S^{-p}\right]\leq\frac{\rho^{2p}}{\eta^{2p}}\\
,M^{p}

where
\sigma_{1}
is the largest singular value of
A
,
T:=\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\sim Beta\left(\frac{n-d}{2},\frac{d}{2}\right)
, and
S:=\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\sim Beta\left(\frac{d-n}{2},\frac{n}{2}\right)
.

Proof.

Recall our SVD
{\bm{A}}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}
and that

\gamma_{1}=\eta^{2}\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}+\xi^{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\gamma_{2}=\eta^{2}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}+\xi^{2}.

Then we have that

\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{{\bm{b}}_{i}^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\|{\bm{b}}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}

Similarly,

\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{{\bm{a}}_{i}^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\|{\bm{a}}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}

Thus, we see that

\gamma_{1}\geq\eta^{2}\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\gamma_{2}\geq\eta^{2}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}.

\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}
depends only on
{\bm{V}}
and is independent of
{\bm{U}},{\bm{\Sigma}}
.
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
depends only on
{\bm{U}}
and is independent of
{\bm{V}},{\bm{\Sigma}}
.
\sigma_{1}
depends only on
{\bm{\Sigma}}
and is independent of
{\bm{U}},{\bm{V}}
. Therefore,
\sigma_{1}
is independent of
T:=\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}
and of
S:=\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
.

Thus, we get that

\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{p}}\leq\frac{1}{\eta^{2p}}\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2p}}{\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2p}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{p}}\leq\frac{1}{\eta^{2p}}\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2p}}{\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2p}}

Then taking the expectation and using the independence, we get that

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{p}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\eta^{2p}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2p}}\right]\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{1}^{2p}\right]\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{p}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\eta^{2p}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2p}}\right]\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma_{1}^{2p}\right]

For
c<1
(where
d<n
), the right null space of
{\bm{A}}
(dimension
n-d
) is a uniformly random
(n-d)
-dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The squared norm
\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}
represents the squared length of the projection of the fixed unit vector
{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
onto this random subspace. The distribution of such a squared projection norm is
\mathrm{Beta}\left(\frac{n-d}{2},\frac{d}{2}\right)
, as it can be represented as the ratio of two independent chi-squared random variables:
\sum_{i=1}^{n-d}G_{i}^{2}/\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{i}^{2}
, where
G_{i}{\sim}N(0,1)
IID, which follows the desired Beta distribution. Similarly for
c>1
.

Since the eigenvalue distribution converges to the compactly supported distribution. We can see that for sufficiently large
n,d
, we have that there exists an
M\geq 1
such that
\sigma_{1}\leq\rho M
almost surely.

For
Y\sim\mathrm{Beta}(\alpha,\beta)
and
p<\alpha
,

\mathbb{E}[Y^{-p}]=\frac{\Gamma(\alpha-p)\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta)}{\Gamma(\alpha)\,\Gamma(\alpha+\beta-p)}.

Moreover, using Stirling on the
\Gamma
ratio,

\mathbb{E}[T^{-p}]\to_{n,d\to\infty}\left(\frac{\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}\right)^{p}=\left(\frac{1}{1-c}\right)^{p}\quad(c<1),

and

\mathbb{E}[S^{-p}]\to_{n,d\to\infty}\left(\frac{\alpha_{2}+\beta_{2}}{\alpha_{2}}\right)^{p}=\left(\frac{c}{c-1}\right)^{p}\quad(c>1).

Thus, there is an
M
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{p}}\right]\leq\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta}\right)^{2p}\,M^{p}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{p}}\right]\leq\left(\frac{\rho}{\eta}\right)^{2p}\,M^{p}

∎

Lemma 19

(Moments of
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{i}
)
.

We have:

(i)

For
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{1}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]=\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(ii)

For
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\right]=\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Proof.

By Lemmas
32
and
16
, the expectation of
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{1}
can be computed by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{1}/\eta^{2}]}1+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}d}\right)=\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right).

By Lemmas
33
and
16
, the variance of
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{1}
can be computed by:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\gamma_{1}/\eta^{2}]^{4}}O\left(\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\right)+o\left(\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\eta^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho^{8}\eta^{8}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{4}}O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+o\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\quad\text{by the scalings of $\eta$ and $\rho$}.

The proof is similar for the other term.
∎

Lemma 20

(Moments of
\eta^{4}/\gamma_{i}^{2}
)
.

We have:

(i)

For
\eta^{4}/\gamma_{1}^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right]=\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(ii)

For
\eta^{4}/\gamma_{2}^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]=\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o\left(1\right),\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Proof.

The expectation of
\eta^{4}/\gamma_{1}^{2}
can be computed by Lemma
19
. By definition we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right]=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]\right)^{2}+\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)=\left(\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)^{2}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

The variance follows Lemma
33
and Lemma
17
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

since the mean is
O(1)
.

The proof is similar for the other term.
∎

Lemma 21
.

Suppose
{\bm{\varepsilon}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose entries have mean
0
, variance
\tau_{\varepsilon}
, and follow our noise assumptions. Then for any indepedent random matrix
{\bm{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varepsilon}},{\bm{Q}}}\left[{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}\right]=\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Tr({\bm{Q}})\right].

Proof.

We have that

{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j}Q_{ij}.

We take the expectation of this sum. By the independence assumption and assumption
\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j}]=0\ \text{when}\ i\neq j
, we then have

\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{\varepsilon}},{\bm{Q}}}\left[{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\varepsilon_{i}^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[Q_{ii}\right]=\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q_{ii}\right]=\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\Tr({\bm{Q}})\right].

∎

D.5
Step 4: Bounding the Expectation of Products of Dependent Terms

In Section
D.2
we decomposed the error into four terms – Bias, Variance, Data Noise and Target alignment. In Section
D.3
, we wrote each of these terms as the sum and product of various “elementary building blocks”. In Section
D.4
, we should that these elementary building blocks concentrate. In this section, since we have tight concentration (i.e., the higher moment bounds). We can use Lemma
36
and Lemma
37
, which shows that the expectation of the product can be approximated by the product of the expectations. In this section, we do that calculation for our different terms.

D.5.1
Step 4: Bias

We begin with the bias term. Recall that for
c<1
, the expected bias by Lemma
5
is equal to

\mathbb{E}[\textbf{Bias}]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left[\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{1}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right]^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\|{\bm{p}}_{1}\|^{2}\right],

where the cross term equals
0
due to
{\bm{\varepsilon}}
having mean zero entries. These two remaining expectations are given by Lemmas
22
,
23
, informally via:

\text{Lemma \ref{lem:bias_term_one}}\ \ +\ \ \tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\times\text{Lemma \ref{lem:p_expectation}}.

For
c<1
, we can plug in the value to get that the expected first term is given by

\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right]^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\right)

and the second is given by

\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\frac{c}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)\right).

Adding them, we then have the desired result:

\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\tilde{n}}\left(\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right]^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\,\frac{c}{1-c}\,\frac{1}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}\right)+o\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{n}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\eta}{n^{2}}\right).

For
c>1
, we instead have the following expanson:

\underbrace{{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}}_{{\bm{t}}_{1}}-\underbrace{\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}}_{{\bm{t}}_{2}}+\underbrace{\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\eta}\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{p}}_{2}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}}_{{\bm{t}}_{3}}

The bias equals the expectation of the norm of this vector. Taking the Frobenius norm, we have the six terms. Among the cross-terms,
\langle{\bm{t}}_{1},{\bm{t}}_{3}\rangle
and
\langle{\bm{t}}_{2},{\bm{t}}_{3}\rangle
have zero mean since
{\bm{t}}_{3}
contains
{\bm{\varepsilon}}
whose entries have mean
0
. We now look at the other terms

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{t}}_{3}\|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\eta}\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{p}}_{2}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{2}\|^{2}\right]\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{lem:eQe}}

The expectation is given by Lemma
23
. Subsequently, Lemmas
22
,
24
,
25
give
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}_{1}\||^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}_{2}\||^{2}]
,
\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{t}}_{1},{\bm{t}}_{3}\rangle]
respectively. Informally, we can compute the bias via:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\textbf{Bias}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}-\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}+\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{\eta}\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}{\bm{p}}_{2}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}_{1}\||^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}_{2}\||^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{t}}_{3}\||^{2}]-2\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{t}}_{1},{\bm{t}}_{3}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle=\text{Lemma \ref{lem:bias_term_one}}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\text{Lemma \ref{lem:p_expectation}}+\text{Lemma \ref{lem:bias_norm_2}}-2\times\text{Lemma \ref{lem:bias_zero_exp}}.

Similar to
c<1
, adding them together and dividing by
\tilde{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\tilde{n}}\left[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left((\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})^{2}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\right)^{2}+\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\left(\frac{c-1}{c}\right)\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+\frac{\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+o\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{n}}\right)+O\left(\frac{\eta}{n^{2}}\right).

D.5.2
Step 4: Variance

Recall that for the variance, we have the following expression (Section
D.3.2
).

\displaystyle\hskip 10.0pt\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\tilde{n}}\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{int}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{A}}}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\tilde{\tau}^{2}\alpha_{z}^{2}}{d}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{Z}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\frac{\tilde{\tau}^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}}{d}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2\tilde{\tau}^{2}\alpha_{A}\alpha_{z}}{d}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\frac{\tilde{\tau}^{2}}{d}{\bm{\varepsilon}}^{\top}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{\varepsilon}}\right].

In particular that the expectation will be the weighted sum of the expressions from Lemmas
26
,
27
,
28
,
29
. Informally,

\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\left(\alpha_{Z}^{2}\times\text{Lemma \ref{lem:interaction_ZZ}}+2\alpha_{Z}\alpha_{A}\times\text{Lemma \ref{lem:interaction_AZ}}+\alpha_{A}^{2}\times\text{Lemma \ref{lem:interaction_AA}}+\text{Lemma \ref{lem:epsilon_term}}\right).

This yields that for
c<1
, after simplification, the variance is

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\left[\alpha_{A}^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}+({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})^{2}\frac{\eta^{2}(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}\frac{c^{2}}{1-c}+2\alpha_{A}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\frac{\eta^{2}c}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\frac{c}{1-c}\frac{d}{\rho^{2}}-\frac{\eta^{2}}{\rho^{2}(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})}\frac{c^{2}}{1-c}\right)\right]+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

For
c>1
, we similarly simplify it to:

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\left[\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\left(\frac{\alpha_{A}^{2}}{c}-\frac{\alpha_{A}^{2}}{d}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\right)+({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\frac{c}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\frac{d}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}-\frac{\eta^{2}}{\rho^{2}(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})}\frac{c}{c-1}\right)\right]+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

D.5.3
Step 4: Data Noise

Recall that for the data noise, we have the following expression

\frac{\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}

Noting that
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}=\Theta(1)
, we see that this term has no more randomness and we do not need to estimate anything.

D.5.4
Step 4: Target Alignment

Recall from Section
D.3.4
that the alignment is given by

-\frac{2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha_{z}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{Z}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+\alpha_{A}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

From
Lemma

30
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{Z}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{\eta^{2}c}{\rho^{2}+\eta^{2}c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)&c<1\\
\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)&c>1\end{cases}.

and from
Lemma

31
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=\begin{cases}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta^{2}c}{\rho^{2}+\eta^{2}c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),&c<1\\
\frac{1}{c}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\left(\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}+\frac{1}{c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right)+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),&c>1\end{cases}.

Thus for
c<1
, the entire interaction term now becomes

-\frac{2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\left(\alpha_{A}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}+(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\,({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\,\frac{\eta^{2}c}{\rho^{2}+\eta^{2}c}+o(1)\right).

For
c>1
, instead we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\tilde{\rho}^{2}}{d}\left(\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{d}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}+\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)\right).

D.5.5
Bias: Helper Lemmas

Lemma 22
.

In the same setting as
Section

2
, we have that for
c<1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{1}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right)^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right]^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\right).

For
c>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\!\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\right]^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\right).

Proof.

For
c<1
, we first expand the square and get:

\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{1}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right)^{2}=(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})^{2}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\frac{\eta^{2}\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})^{2}+\frac{2}{\eta}\frac{\eta\xi}{\gamma_{1}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}).

By Lemmas
9
and
20
, then we see that, using the square root of the covariance to bound the difference between the expectation of the product and the product of the expectation.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{2}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta\xi}{\gamma_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\eta}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\rho^{2}\eta}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Combining these terms together, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{1}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right)^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})^{2}+\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{2}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{2\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta}\left(\frac{\rho^{2}\eta}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)\right)(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\right]^{2}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\eta n}\right)\right).

We now consider
c>1
. Recalling that
\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}=\tilde{\eta}{\bm{u}}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}
, we let
c_{1}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}
and expand:

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{2}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2}\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\tilde{\eta}^{2}\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}((\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}+2c_{1}\tilde{\eta}^{2}\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}.

Not that for the second and third terms, we have that
\xi,\gamma_{2}
only depend on the singular values of
{\bm{A}}
and the rest only depend on the singular vectors. Hence, these terms are independent.

First note that when
d>n
, the number of singular values equals
n
, which is less than the dimension
d
. As a result,

{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}={\bm{U}}{\bm{\Sigma}}{\bm{V}}^{\top}{\bm{V}}{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\dagger}{\bm{U}}^{\top}={\bm{U}}\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{I}}_{n\times n}\quad\bm{0}_{n\times(d-n)}\\
\bm{0}_{(d-n)\times n}\quad\bm{0}_{(d-n)\times(d-n)}\end{bmatrix}{\bm{U}}^{\top}.

Then we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}})_{i}^{2}\right]=\frac{n}{d}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{c}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

(13)

since
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{U}}
is a uniformly random vector of length
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
after the rotation
{\bm{U}}
.

For the middle term, by Proposition
2
and the above Equation
13
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}((\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\alpha_{Z}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}-2\alpha_{A}\alpha_{Z}\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]+\alpha_{A}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1).

Similarly, for the last term, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}){\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1).

Thus putting these expectations together, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[c_{1}^{2}+\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)^{2}+2c_{1}\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[c_{1}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\right]^{2}\right].

Similar to the
c<1
case, we take the expectation for terms involving
\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}
and get:

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[\left((\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\frac{\rho^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\!\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\right)^{\!2}+o\!\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\right)+O\!\left(\frac{1}{\eta n}\right)\right].

∎

Lemma 23

(Expectations involving
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
)
.

In the setting of
Section

2
, we have that

1.

For
c=d/n<1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{1}\|^{2}\right]=\frac{c}{1-c}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right).

2.

For
c=d/n>1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{2}\|^{2}\right]=\frac{\eta^{2}}{c-1}\,\frac{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right).

Proof.

First, Lemma
6
tells us that

\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{1}\|^{2}=\frac{\eta^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}.

Then recall from Lemma
9
that

\mathbb{E}[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}]=\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}(\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2})=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)

and Lemma
19
tells us

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]=\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{i}}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

Again Section
D.4.2
tells us that the assumption of Lemma
37
is satisfied and that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{1}\|^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{1}}\right)\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{1-c}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{1-c}\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right).

Using Lemma
6
for
{\bm{p}}_{2}
,

\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{2}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\left(\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}+2\eta^{3}\xi\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}+\eta^{2}\xi^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right).

To begin, we start estimating

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right].

Using our Spherical Hypercontractivity, we have that
\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}
and
{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}
satisfy the assumptions for Lemma
36
. Then using Lemmas
9
and
10
we first have that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]=1-\frac{1}{c}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{8}d}\right).

Thus, using Lemma
37
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]+O\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{d},\frac{1}{\rho^{8}d}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c^{3}}{(c-1)^{3}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

and using Lemma
36
, since all the means are
O(1)
, we have that

\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right)=O\left(\max\left(\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right),\mathrm{Var}\left({\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right)\right)\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Then Lemma
20
gives mean and variance of
\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}
. Since
\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}
does not satisfy the higher moment bound, and cannot be directly included in the product, we can include it via the classical bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}{\bm{h}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right)\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)}

(14)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\frac{c}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

(15)

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

(16)

Similarly, we can do the same thing for the other term. For the middle term we note that from Lemma
11

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left({\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{6}d}\right)

and Lemma
9
tells us

\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]=1-\frac{1}{c}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right]=\frac{1}{\eta}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)

Thus using Lemma
37
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]=0+O\left(\frac{1}{d}\right)

Thus using the standard covariance bound for the expectation of product versus product of expectation, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{3}\xi\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=0+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

For the last term, we have that, using Lemma
37

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)

and from Lemma
36

\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)

Then using the standard bound, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}\xi^{2}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\|{\bm{k}}\|^{2}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}\frac{1}{c-1}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{4}n}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right).

Finally, putting all three terms together we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\|{\bm{p}}_{2}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)+\frac{1}{c-1}\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta^{2}}{c-1}\,\frac{\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right).

∎

From the above proofs, we make an important observation that the individual terms from Lemmas
9
,
10
,
11
,
16
all have means
O(1)
and variances
O(1/n)
. Hence, by Lemma
36
, we can bound the variance of a product of terms by
O(1/n)
, given that these terms satisfy the lemma assumptions. Essentially, only
\eta^{2}/\gamma_{i}
and
\eta^{4}/\gamma_{i}^{2}
fail the assumption on higher moment bound, so we deal with them via the classical bound after carrying out the product. This simplification ensures proper concentration and will be used at times in the following proofs without reference.

D.5.6
Variance: Helper Lemmas

Lemma 24
.

In the setting of
Section

2
, we have that for
c>1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\right\|^{2}\right]=\tilde{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\|\beta_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\left(\frac{c-1}{c}\right)\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Proof.

Since
\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}=\tilde{\eta}{\bm{u}}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\eta^{2}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}.

Similar to last lemma, using Lemmas
37
,
9
,
10
,
20
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]\right)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\rho^{2}+\eta^{2})^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\frac{c}{\rho^{2}(c-1)}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}d}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\left(\frac{c-1}{c}\right)\frac{\eta^{4}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Hence, it directly follows from here that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{s}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{h}}^{\top}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\|\beta_{*}\|^{2}}{d}\left(\frac{c-1}{c}\right)\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

∎

Lemma 25
.

In the setting of
Section

2
, we have that for
c>1
:

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}\left[(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}){\bm{I}}+\frac{\xi}{\gamma_{2}}(\alpha_{Z}{\bm{I}}-\alpha_{A}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger})\right]\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=O\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\right).

Proof.

Using
\tilde{{\bm{Z}}}=\tilde{\eta}{\bm{u}}\tilde{{\bm{v}}}^{\top}
, we can expand this into three terms. We can take expectations in a similar way via Lemmas
37
,
9
,
10
,
11
: Let
c_{1}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}
. Each term contains a zero expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}c_{1}\frac{\eta\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta}c_{1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta}c_{1}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{2}}{\gamma_{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)=O\left(\frac{\eta}{n}\right).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{Z}\frac{\eta\xi\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{Z}\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{Z}\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\eta}^{2}\alpha_{A}\frac{\eta\xi\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{Z}\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi}{\eta}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{s}}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{A}}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{u}}{\bm{h}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha_{Z}\tilde{\eta}^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\left(\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

Thus the cross term concentrates around zero at a rate of
O(\eta/n)
.
∎

Lemma 26
.

In the same setting as
Section

2
, we have that

\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{Z}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\right]=\begin{cases}\frac{\eta^{2}(\eta^{2}+\rho^{2})}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}\frac{c^{2}}{1-c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)&c<1\\
\frac{\eta^{2}}{\eta^{2}+\rho^{2}}\frac{c}{c-1}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)&c>1\end{cases}.

Proof.

We start with
c<1
and expand this term using Proposition
1
:

{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{Z}}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger}({\bm{Z}}+{\bm{A}})^{\dagger\top}{\bm{Z}}{\bm{\beta}}_{*}=\frac{\eta^{2}\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{t}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}({\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}})({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\frac{2\eta^{3}\|{\bm{t}}\|^{2}\xi}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{h}}^{\top}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}.

We then start plugging in the expectations of these three terms and the “cumulative" variance of the sum according to Lemma
37
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{2}\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}\xi^{2}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi^{2}}{\eta^{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{h}}\|^{2}\right]+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}\right)O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)}

\displaystyle=({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\frac{\rho^{4}\eta^{4}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}+o(1)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}n}\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\frac{c^{2}}{1-c}+o\left(\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\right)\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2}}{(\eta^{2}c+\rho^{2})^{2}}\frac{c^{2}}{1-c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+o(1)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\eta^{4}\|{\bm{t}}\|^{4}}{\gamma_{1}^{2}}({\bm{k}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger}{\bm{A}}^{\dagger\top}{\bm{k}})({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\right]
