be real random variables with means
a=\mathbb{E}\left[A\right]
,
b=\mathbb{E}\left[B\right]
and finite variances. Assume

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(A-a\right)^{4}\right]\leq K_{A}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)^{2},\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(B-b\right)^{4}\right]\leq K_{B}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)^{2}.

Then, with
C_{4}:=(K_{A}K_{B})^{1/4}
,

\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)}\;\leq\;|a|\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)}\;+\;|b|\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)}\;+\;C_{4}\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)}.

Moreover, as
\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right),\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\to 0
,

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)=O\left(a^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right)+O\left(b^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\right)+o\!\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right).

It directly follows that if all the means are
O(1)
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right)+O\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\right).

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\!\left(ABC\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(C\right)\right)+O\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right)+O\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\right)\quad\text{and so on by induction}.

Proof.

Write

AB-ab=a\,\tilde{B}+b\,\tilde{A}+\tilde{A}\tilde{B}.

Using
\mathrm{Var}(U+V)=\mathrm{Var}(U)+\mathrm{Var}(V)+2\,\mathrm{Cov}(U,V)
and

|\mathrm{Cov}(U,V)|\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(U)\mathrm{Var}(V)}
, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Var}\!\left(a\tilde{B}+b\tilde{A}+\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\left(\,|a|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{B}\right)}\;+\;|b|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\right)}\;+\;\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)}\,\right)^{\!2}.

Since
\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{A})=\mathrm{Var}(A)
and
\mathrm{Var}(\tilde{B})=\mathrm{Var}(B)
, it remains to bound

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)
. By Cauchy–Schwarz (Hölder with
p=q=2
),

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}\!\left[\tilde{A}^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\right]\;\leq\;\left(\mathbb{E}\!\left[\tilde{A}^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\!\left[\tilde{B}^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}.

Since we assume fourth–moment control

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\tilde{A}^{4}\right]\leq K_{A}\,\mathrm{Var}(A)^{2}
and

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\tilde{B}^{4}\right]\leq K_{B}\,\mathrm{Var}(B)^{2}
, then

\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)}\;\leq\;(K_{A}K_{B})^{1/4}\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(A)\mathrm{Var}(B)}.

Hence

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)\;\leq\;\Big(\,|a|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(B)}\;+\;|b|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(A)}\;+\;C_{4}\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(A)\mathrm{Var}(B)}\,\Big)^{\!2},\qquad C_{4}:=(K_{A}K_{B})^{1/4}.

For the moreover part, using the exact variance–covariance expansion,

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)=a^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)+b^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+2ab\,\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(A,B\right)+\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)+2a\,\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(\tilde{B},\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)+2b\,\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(\tilde{A},\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right),

we bound the three remainder terms using Cauchy–Schwarz and the fourth–moment control:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{A}^{2}\tilde{B}^{2}\right]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{A}^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{B}^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}\leq C_{4}^{2}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right),

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(\tilde{B},\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{B}\right)}\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)}\leq C_{4}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)},

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(\tilde{A},\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\right)}\,\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\tilde{A}\tilde{B}\right)}\leq C_{4}\,\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)}.

As
\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right),\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\to 0
, each of these is

o\!\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right)
.

For the covariance term, Cauchy–Schwarz and the inequality

2uv\leq\varepsilon u^{2}+\varepsilon^{-1}v^{2}
(for any
\varepsilon>0
) with

u:=|a|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)}
,
v:=|b|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)}
give

\left|\,2ab\,\mathrm{Cov}\!\left(A,B\right)\right|\leq 2|ab|\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)}\leq\varepsilon\,a^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)+\varepsilon^{-1}b^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right).

Therefore,

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)\leq(1+\varepsilon)\,a^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)+(1+\varepsilon^{-1})\,b^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+o\!\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right).

Choosing, e.g.,
\varepsilon=1
yields

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(AB\right)=O\!\left(a^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right)+O\!\left(b^{2}\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)\right)+o\!\left(\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\right)+\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\right)\right),

which proves the moreover statement.
∎

Lemma 36

(Variance of general product)
.

Let
m\geq 2
and let
X_{1},\dots,X_{m}
be real random variables with nonzero means
\mu_{i}:=\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\neq 0
and variances
f_{i}(n):=\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.
Assume that for some integer
M\geq m
(it is enough to take
M=m
),

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\,|X_{i}-\mu_{i}|^{2M}\,\right]\;=\;O\!\left(\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})^{M}\right)\qquad\text{for each }i=1,\dots,m.

(32)

Then

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(\,\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\,\right)\;=\;O\!\left(\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\Big)^{2}\right)\;=\;O\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq m}f_{i}(n)\right).

Proof.

Write
\Delta_{i}:=X_{i}-\mu_{i}
so that
\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{i}]=0
and
\|\Delta_{i}\|_{L_{2}}=\sigma_{i}
.
By assumption Equation
32
with
M\geq m
and monotonicity of
L_{p}
norms,

\|\Delta_{i}\|_{L_{2k}}\;=\;O\left(\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\right)\qquad\text{for every }1\leq k\leq m,\ i=1,\dots,m.

Expand the product multilinearly:

\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mu_{i}=\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[m]}\ \left(\prod_{j\in S^{c}}\mu_{j}\right)\ \left(\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right).

Taking
L_{2}
norms and using the triangle inequality,

\left\|\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mu_{i}\right\|_{L_{2}}\ \leq\ \sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[m]}\ \left(\prod_{j\in S^{c}}|\mu_{j}|\right)\ \left\|\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right\|_{L_{2}}.

For a fixed nonempty
S
with
|S|=k
, apply Hölder with exponents all equal to
2k
:

\left\|\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right\|_{L_{2}}\ \leq\ \prod_{i\in S}\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{L_{2k}}\ =\ O\!\left(\prod_{i\in S}\sqrt{f_{i}}\right),

where we used
\|\Delta_{i}\|_{L_{2k}}=O(\sqrt{f_{i}})
for
k\leq m
.

Let
c_{i}:=\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}
. Summing over subsets
S
shows

\left\|\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mu_{i}\right\|_{L_{2}}\ \leq\ A\,\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{m}(1+c_{i})-1\Big)\ \leq\ A\,(e^{\Xi}-1),

where
\Xi:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i}
and
A
is a constant depending only on
m
,
\{\mu_{i}\}
, and the moment constants (not on
n
).
Hence

\mathrm{Var}\!\Big(\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\Big)\ \leq\ \left\|\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mu_{i}\right\|_{L_{2}}^{\,2}\ =\ O(\Xi^{2})\ =\ O\!\left(\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\Big)^{2}\right).

Since
m
is fixed,
(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sqrt{f_{i}})^{2}\leq m^{2}\max_{i}f_{i}
, giving the claimed bound.
∎

Corollary 1

(Higher moments of the centered product)
.

Fix
p\geq 1
. Under the hypotheses of Lemma
36
, then

\Big\|\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\Big\|_{L_{2p}}\;\leq\;C_{p,m}\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[m]}\ \Big(\prod_{j\in S^{c}}|\mathbb{E}[X_{j}]|\Big)\ \prod_{i\in S}\sqrt{f_{i}}\;=\;o(1),

and hence
\mathbb{E}\big|\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}-\mathbb{E}\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}\big|^{2p}=o(1)
.

Lemma 37

(Expectation of Product vs. Product of Expectations)
.

Fix
k\geq 2
. Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{k}
be random variables. Assume:

1.

Uniformly bounded means:
\sup_{n,i}|\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]|\leq M<\infty
.

2.

Vanishing variances:
\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})=f_{i}(n)
with
f_{i}(n)\to 0
as
n\to\infty
for each
i
.

3.

Moment control up to order
k
: For each
i
and every
p\in\{2,\dots,k\}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]\right|^{p}\right]\ \leq\ C_{p}\;\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})^{p/2},

with constants
C_{p}
.

Then for finite
k
, we have:

\left|\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}\right]-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{i}\,\right|\ =\ O\!\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\right)^{2}\right)=O\!\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq k}f_{i}(n)\right).

Proof.

Set
\Delta_{i}:=X_{i}-\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]
, so
\mathbb{E}\Delta_{i}=0
,
\mathrm{Var}(X_{i})=\mathrm{Var}(\Delta_{i})=f_{i}(n)
, and by assumption

\left\|\Delta_{i}\right\|_{L_{p}}:=\left(\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta_{i}|^{p}\right]\right)^{1/p}\ \leq\ C_{p}^{1/p}\,f_{i}(n)^{1/2},\qquad p=2,\dots,k.

Using the multilinearity of expectation,

\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}\left(\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]+\Delta_{i}\right)=\sum_{S\subseteq[k]}\left(\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right)\left(\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}]\right),

Thus,

\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]=\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[k]}\left[\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right]\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}].

Then taking the expectation and noting that
\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}]
is a constant, we get

\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}\right]-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}[X_{i}]=\sum_{\emptyset\neq S\subseteq[k]}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right]\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}].

If
S=\{\ell\}
then
\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right]=\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{\ell}]=0
. Hence every singleton term vanishes exactly, and the sum begins at
|S|=2
. From the bounded means assumption,

\left|\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}]\right|\ \leq\ M^{\,k-|S|},\qquad\forall S\subseteq[k].

Fix a nonempty subset
S
with
|S|=m\geq 2
. By generalized Hölder with all exponents equal to
m
(so
\sum_{i\in S}\tfrac{1}{m}=1
),

\left|\mathbb{E}\!\left[\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right]\right|\leq\prod_{i\in S}\|\Delta_{i}\|_{L_{m}}\leq\prod_{i\in S}\left(C_{m}^{1/m}\,f_{i}(n)^{1/2}\right)=C_{m}\prod_{i\in S}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}.

Therefore, for every
S
with
|S|=m\geq 2
,

\left|\mathbb{E}\!\left[\prod_{i\in S}\Delta_{i}\right]\prod_{j\notin S}\mathbb{E}X_{j}\right|\leq M^{\,k-m}\,C_{m}\prod_{i\in S}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}.

Let
c_{i}:=\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\geq 0
. Denote by

e_{m}(c_{1},\dots,c_{k}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[k]\\
|S|=m\end{subarray}}\ \prod_{i\in S}c_{i}

the
m
-th elementary symmetric polynomial. Summing the bound from, we get

\left|\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}\right]-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{i}\,\right|\leq\sum_{m=2}^{k}M^{\,k-m}C_{m}\,e_{m}(c_{1},\dots,c_{k}).

Let
M_{\star}:=\max_{2\leq m\leq k}M^{\,k-m}C_{m}
. Since
e_{m}\geq 0
for
c_{i}\geq 0
,

\sum_{m=2}^{k}M^{\,k-m}C_{m}\,e_{m}\ \leq\ M_{\star}\sum_{m=2}^{k}e_{m}(c_{1},\dots,c_{k}).

Recall the identity

\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+c_{i})=\sum_{m=0}^{k}e_{m}(c_{1},\dots,c_{k})=1+\sum_{m=1}^{k}e_{m}(c_{1},\dots,c_{k}),

so that
\sum_{m=2}^{k}e_{m}=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+c_{i})-1-\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{i}
. Hence

\left|\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}\right]-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{i}\,\right|\leq M_{\star}\!\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+c_{i})-1-\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{i}\right).

Let
\Xi:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{i}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Since
\log(1+u)\leq u
for
u\geq 0
,

\prod_{i=1}^{k}(1+c_{i})=\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\log(1+c_{i})\right)\leq\exp(\Xi).

Thus, the difference is at most
M_{\star}(e^{\Xi}-1-\Xi)
.
By Taylor’s theorem,
e^{\Xi}=1+\Xi+\frac{1}{2}\Xi^{2}e^{\xi}
for some
\xi\in[0,\Xi]
, so
e^{\Xi}-1-\Xi=\frac{1}{2}\Xi^{2}e^{\xi}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Xi^{2}e^{\Xi}
(since
\xi\leq\Xi
and
e^{\xi}\leq e^{\Xi}
).
Therefore,

\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}\right]-\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}X_{i}\right|\leq\frac{M_{\star}}{2}\Xi^{2}e^{\Xi}=O(\Xi^{2}),

as
\Xi\to 0
and
e^{\Xi}\to 1
. Since
\Xi=O\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sqrt{f_{i}(n)}\right)
, we get the result.
∎

Lemma 38

(Moment preservation under monomial
\leftrightarrow
Hermite change of basis)
.

Fix
M\in\mathbb{N}
and degree
r\in\mathbb{N}
. Let

\mathcal{M}:=\{x^{\gamma}:\ \gamma\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\ |\gamma|\leq r\},\qquad\mathcal{H}:=\{{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}:\ \alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{M},\ |\alpha|\leq r\},

with
{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)=\prod_{j=1}^{M}H_{\alpha_{j}}(x_{j})
the probabilists’ Hermite basis.
For any (random) coefficients
\{a_{\gamma}\}_{|\gamma|\leq r}
define the random polynomial

P(x)=\sum_{|\gamma|\leq r}a_{\gamma}\,x^{\gamma}
. Then there is a deterministic, invertible matrix

T=T(M,r)
such that the Hermite coefficients
c=\{c_{\alpha}\}_{|\alpha|\leq r}
in

P(x)=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq r}c_{\alpha}\,{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)
satisfy

c\;=\;T\,a.

Consequently, for any
p\geq 1
,

\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{p}}\;\leq\;\sum_{|\gamma|\leq r}|T_{\alpha\gamma}|\,\|a_{\gamma}\|_{L_{p}}\quad\text{for all }\alpha,

so if each
a_{\gamma}\in L_{p}
then each
c_{\alpha}\in L_{p}
.
Moreover, since
T
is invertible, the converse also holds: if each
c_{\alpha}\in L_{p}
then each

a_{\gamma}\in L_{p}
.

Proof.

In one dimension, each monomial admits a finite Hermite expansion

x^{m}=\sum_{j=0}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}t_{m,j}\,H_{m-2j}(x)
with deterministic coefficients

t_{m,j}
; in several dimensions, take tensor products to obtain

x^{\gamma}=\sum_{|\alpha|\leq|\gamma|}T_{\alpha\gamma}\,{\bm{H}}_{\alpha}(x)
.
Ordering multi-indices by total degree yields a block upper-triangular, deterministic,
invertible matrix
T=T(M,r)
. Linearity gives
c=Ta
. For
p\geq 1
, Minkowski’s inequality
yields

\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{p}}=\big\|\sum_{\gamma}T_{\alpha\gamma}a_{\gamma}\big\|_{L_{p}}\leq\sum_{\gamma}|T_{\alpha\gamma}|\,\|a_{\gamma}\|_{L_{p}}
,
so finiteness of all
\|a_{\gamma}\|_{L_{p}}
implies finiteness of all
\|c_{\alpha}\|_{L_{p}}
.
Invertibility gives the converse using
a=T^{-1}c
and the same argument with
T^{-1}
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Specific Cases and Overfitting

F.1
Proof of Theorem
1
.

Proof.

We set
\alpha_{Z}=\alpha_{A}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{A}=\alpha
,
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
,
\tilde{\tau}=\tau
in the above Theorem
5
and note that it
greatly simplifies each term. Algebra shows that for
c<1

\text{Bias}=\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{c}{1-c}\frac{\theta^{2}}{d(\theta^{2}+\tau^{2})},\quad\text{ Variance}=\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{c}{1-c}\left[1-\frac{\theta^{2}}{d(\theta^{2}+\tau^{2})}\right],

\text{Data Noise}=\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},\quad\text{ Target Alignment}=-2\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

While for
c>1
, we can first send
d,n\to\infty
and many terms become asymptotically
0
. In the end, we get that:

\text{Bias}=\alpha^{2}{\theta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\tau^{2}c}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}\right)^{2},\quad\text{Data Noise}=\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

\text{Variance}=\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\frac{1}{c}+\alpha^{2}\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\frac{1}{c-1}.

\text{ Target Alignment}=-2\alpha^{2}{\tau}^{2}\left(\left(1-\frac{1}{c}\right)\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\frac{1}{c}\right),

Adding these terms together, we see with simple algebra that many terms cancel or can be combined, establishing the stated formula.
∎

F.2
Proof of Theorem
2
.

Proof.

We set
\alpha_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}
,
\alpha_{A}=\tilde{\alpha}_{A}
,
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
,
\tilde{\tau}=\tau
, and send
d,n\to\infty
in Theorem
5
. Recall that
\Delta_{c}=\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}
and
\Delta_{1}=\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A}
. Then some algebra shows that for
c<1
,

\text{Bias}=\theta^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\Delta_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}}\right)^{2},\quad\text{Data Noise}=\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

\text{ Target Alignment}=-2\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\ -2\alpha_{A}\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\Delta_{1}\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}},

\text{Variance}=\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}+\tau^{2}_{\varepsilon}\frac{c}{1-c}+\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left[\frac{1}{1-c}\frac{\theta^{4}+\theta^{2}\tau^{2}c}{(\theta^{2}+\tau^{2})^{2}}\Delta_{1}^{2}+2\alpha_{A}\Delta_{1}\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}}\right].

For
c>1
, we have that

\text{Bias}=\theta^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\Delta_{c}^{2}\left(\frac{\tau^{2}c}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}\right)^{2},\quad\text{Data Noise}=\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

\text{ Target Alignment}=-2\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}-2\alpha_{A}\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\Delta_{c}\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c},

\text{Variance}=\alpha_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}+\tau^{2}_{\varepsilon}\frac{1}{c-1}+\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\frac{c}{1-c}\frac{\theta^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}\Delta_{c}^{2}.

We proceed by adding these terms together and the results follow from algebra.
∎

F.3
Proof of Theorem
3
.

Proof.

We set
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
and
\tilde{\tau}=\tau
in Theorem
5
and have the regime of equal operator norm
\theta^{2}=\gamma\tau^{2}
. Since we are interested in the limit
c\to\infty
, we only consider the overparameterized case
c>1
. We first take the limit
d,n\to\infty
and have that:

\text{Bias}={\tau}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\sqrt{\gamma}(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{c\sqrt{\gamma}}{\gamma+c}\right)^{2},\quad\text{Data Noise}=\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

\text{Target Alignment}=-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}{\tau}^{2}\left(\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+c}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\alpha_{A}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}\right),

\text{Variance}=\tau^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}+\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\frac{c}{(c-1)}\frac{\gamma}{\gamma+c}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)^{2}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{c-1}\right).

The rest follows from simple calculus: if
\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}\neq\alpha_{Z}
,
\gamma=\omega_{c}(1)
, and
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}\neq 0
, the bias will diverge and other terms are controlled, yielding catastrophic. If
\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}=\alpha_{Z}
,
\omega_{c}(1)\leq\gamma\leq o_{c}(c^{2})
, and
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}}\neq 0
, a similar thing happens. In other cases, all of these terms are controlled and become finite values in the limit
\lim_{c\to\infty}\mathcal{R}_{c}-\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}
, giving us tempered overfitting.

\lim_{c\to\infty}\mathcal{R}_{c}=\begin{cases}\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}&{\bm{\beta}}\perp u\\[5.0pt]
\tau^{2}\left[\gamma\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\right]&{\bm{\beta}}\not\perp u,\gamma=\Theta_{c}(1)\\[5.0pt]
\infty&\alpha_{Z}\neq\tilde{\alpha}_{Z},{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\not\perp u,\gamma=\omega(1)\\[5.0pt]
\infty&\alpha_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z},{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\not\perp u,\omega(1)\leq\gamma\leq o(c^{2})\\[5.0pt]
\tau^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\phi}{(\phi+1)^{2}}\,\alpha_{Z}^{2}-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\alpha_{Z}\right)({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\alpha_{A}^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\right]&\alpha_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z},{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\not\perp u,\gamma=\phi c^{2}\\[5.0pt]
\tau^{2}\left[(\alpha_{Z}^{2}-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\alpha_{Z})({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\alpha_{A}^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\right]&\alpha_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z},{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\not\perp u,\gamma=\omega(c^{2})\end{cases}

∎

F.4
Proof of Theorem
4
.

Proof.

We start with the first part and assume that
\alpha_{Z}\neq\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}
. Similarly, we have that
\tilde{\theta}=\theta
and
\tilde{\tau}=\tau
in Theorem
5
. To achieve equal Frobenius norm, we set
\theta^{2}=d\tau^{2}
and send
d,n\to\infty
so several terms would vanish.

In particular, for
c<1
, we have that

\text{Bias}={\theta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\frac{\tau^{2}}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}}\right)^{2}={\tau}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\sqrt{d}(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+(\alpha_{Z}-\alpha_{A})\frac{\sqrt{d}}{d+1}\right)^{2},

It is clear that this term becomes
\infty
since the term inside the parentheses scales with
d
. Note that the variance and data noise are non-negative, and target alignment is controlled. We have that
\mathcal{R}_{c}=\infty
for
c\in(0,1)
.

For
c>1
, the same logic follows, and we also note that:

\text{Bias}={\theta}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z}+\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{\tau^{2}c}{\theta^{2}+\tau^{2}c}\right)^{2}={\tau}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\sqrt{d}(\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}-\alpha_{Z})+\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{\sqrt{d}c}{d+c}\right)^{2},

which scales with
d
with other terms controlled. Hence,
\mathcal{R}_{c}=\infty
for all
c\neq 1
.

Now assume that
\alpha_{Z}=\tilde{\alpha}_{Z}
. Since we are interested in
c\to\infty
, we only consider
c>1
. First, from algebra and taking the limit for
d,n
, we have that:

\text{Bias}={\tau}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\left(\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)\frac{c\sqrt{d}}{d+c}\right)^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{Data Noise}=\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2},

\text{Target Alignment}=-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}{\tau}^{2}\left(\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\alpha_{A}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}\right),

\text{Variance}=\tau^{2}\alpha_{A}^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}}{c}+\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\frac{c}{(c-1)}\left(\alpha_{Z}-\frac{\alpha_{A}}{c}\right)^{2}+\tau_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{c-1}\right).

We now take
c\to\infty
and many terms vanish in this limit, yielding:

\lim_{c\to\infty}\mathcal{R}_{c}=-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\alpha_{Z}{\tau}^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}+\tau^{2}({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}\alpha_{Z}^{2}+\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\tau^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}=\tau^{2}\left[({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\top}{\bm{u}})^{2}(\alpha_{Z}^{2}-2\tilde{\alpha}_{A}\alpha_{Z})+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}\tilde{\alpha}_{A}^{2}\right].

∎

Proposition 3

(Non–existence of a canceling scale parameter)
.

Let
\alpha_{A},\alpha_{Z}>0
be fixed scalars, let
{\bm{u}},{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be
fixed vectors, and set

a\;:=\|{\bm{\beta}}_{*}\|^{2}>0,\qquad b\;:=\bigl({\bm{\beta}}_{*}^{\!\top}{\bm{u}}\bigr)^{2}\in[0,a].

For every positive real number
\phi
define

f(\phi)\;=\;\alpha_{A}^{2}\,a\;+\;\Bigl(\alpha_{Z}^{2}\!\Bigl(1+\frac{1}{\phi}\Bigr)-2\alpha_{Z}\alpha_{A}\Bigr)\,b.

Then

f(\phi)>0\quad\text{for all }\phi>0.

Consequently the equation
f(\phi)=0
has no solution with

\phi\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

If
b=0
(i.e.
{\bm{\beta}}_{*}
is orthogonal to
{\bm{u}}
) we have

f(\phi)=\alpha_{A}^{2}a>0
, so no positive
\phi
can cancel the
expression. Hence assume
b>0
.

Writing
r:=b/a\in(0,1]
we obtain

f(\phi)=a\Bigl[\alpha_{A}^{2}+\alpha_{Z}(\alpha_{Z}-2\alpha_{A})\,r+\frac{\alpha_{Z}^{2}r}{\phi}\Bigr].

Since
r\leq 1
,

\alpha_{A}^{2}+\alpha_{Z}(\alpha_{Z}-2\alpha_{A})\,r\;\geq\;\alpha_{A}^{2}+\alpha_{Z}(\alpha_{Z}-2\alpha_{A})\;=\;\bigl(\alpha_{A}-\alpha_{Z}\bigr)^{2}\;\geq 0.

Thus the square bracket in
(\ast)
is the sum of a non–negative
term and a strictly positive term.

∎
